# Form 4 Combined Science — Image Brief & Generation Prompts

Every illustration referenced by the Form 4 Combined Science lessons, with the **exact file name to save it as** and an **unambiguous drawing prompt**.

The file names are already wired into the code, so as soon as a PNG lands in the folder below it appears in the lesson. Nothing else needs changing. Until a file exists the lesson silently hides that figure (`<Figure>` removes itself on load error), so you can add the artwork in any order without breaking the pages.

---

## 1. Where to save

```
public/images/courses/o-level/combined-science/form-4/
```

- File names are **case-sensitive**, all lowercase, hyphen-separated, and must match exactly.
- Format: **PNG** with a solid white background (not transparent — the lesson cards are white and a transparent PNG will look identical but exports inconsistently to PDF).
- Recommended size: **1600 px wide**, height to suit the composition. Step-by-step images can be **1200 px wide**.
- Keep files under ~400 KB each; run them through an optimiser before committing.

Source files referencing these names:

| Subject | File |
|---|---|
| Biology | `src/features/courses/o-level/form-4/combined-science/LearningOutcome1.tsx` |
| Chemistry | `src/features/courses/o-level/form-4/combined-science/LearningOutcome2.tsx` |
| Physics | `src/features/courses/o-level/form-4/combined-science/LearningOutcome3.tsx` |

---

## 2. House style — reference copy (already folded into every prompt below)

Every individual prompt in this document already has the STYLE, PALETTE and DO NOT INCLUDE rules built directly into it, so you can copy just the one blockquote you need — even into a brand-new chat with no other context — and it will still generate on-model. This section is kept only as the master reference in case you ever need to edit the wording everywhere at once.

> **STYLE:** Clean flat 2D vector textbook illustration for a secondary school science book. Pure white background. Bold, clear black outlines. Flat solid fill colours with no gradients, no drop shadows, no glow, no 3D bevels, no photorealism, no watercolour or sketch texture. Everything drawn straight-on (front elevation or plain cross-section) — **no perspective, no isometric view, no tilted angles**. Objects are well spaced and never overlap a label. All text is horizontal, in a plain sans-serif font, dark charcoal (#1e293b), large enough to read when the image is 700 px wide. Labels connect to what they name with a thin straight leader line ending in a small dot or arrowhead. British/Zimbabwean school spelling. Use SI units with a space before the unit (e.g. `20 N`, `450 °C`). Nothing is cut off at the edges — leave a 4% margin all round.
>
> **PALETTE (internal colour reference only — never render hex codes, colour names, or CSS syntax as visible text in the image):** blue (#3b82f6), dark blue (#1e40af), green (#22c55e), dark green (#15803d), amber (#f59e0b), yellow (#eab308), red (#ef4444), purple (#8b5cf6), grey (#94a3b8), dark slate (#1e293b), off-white (#f1f5f9). Water/aqueous liquid is always light blue (#dbeafe). Glass apparatus is drawn as thin outlines with no fill.
>
> **DO NOT INCLUDE:** watermarks, signatures, logos, borders or frames, decorative background scenery, human faces unless the prompt explicitly asks for a person, arrows or labels not listed in the prompt, invented extra text, lorem ipsum, or misspelled labels, hex codes, colour codes (e.g. "#3b82f6"), or CSS-style notation printed anywhere in the image.

Two extra rules that matter more than they sound (also repeated inline where relevant):

1. **Reproduce label text exactly as quoted.** Where a prompt writes `label it "Upper epidermis"`, that string must appear verbatim. Wrong or misspelled labels are worse than no labels in a revision resource.
2. **Step images in a series must match.** Every image in a `-step1 … -stepN` set must use the same apparatus, the same viewing angle, the same size and the same colours, so a student can see what changed between steps and nothing else.

Draw a large right-angled gradient triangle on the line between the points (2, 10) and (8, 40), with the horizontal side labelled "Δx = 8 − 2 = 6 s" and the vertical side labelled "Δy = 40 − 10 = 30 m". Working written in the empty area of the graph: "Gradient = 30 ÷ 6 = 5 m/s = the speed". Add a callout: "Use the BIGGEST triangle that fits on your line".

## 8. Verifying your work

After dropping files into the folder, check that nothing is missing or misnamed:

```bash
# List file names the code expects but which do not exist yet
cd src/features/courses/o-level/form-4/combined-science
grep -oh "f4Image('[^']*')" LearningOutcome*.tsx \
  | sed "s/f4Image('//;s/')//" | sort -u \
  | while read -r f; do
      [ -f "../../../../../public/images/courses/o-level/combined-science/form-4/$f" ] \
        || echo "MISSING: $f"
    done
```

An empty result means every referenced image is present.
